## 2026-05-14 — Major reframing: Cell/Propagator/Scheduler Orthogonality principle codified; hybrid pivot under reconsideration

User direction post-D.3.EC Group 1 review: "I want to step back and discuss a bit more with you the issue with the off-network cell approach... If we're using off-network scaffolding to optimize, and that we'll need to retire with the PReduce/SH series anyways... PReduce series, we're planning on working through next, so we'd be potentially facing the same sort of concern."

This is a load-bearing reframing. The user is questioning whether the hybrid pivot's "off-network struct field as live state" approach is actually principle-aligned, given that PReduce will face the same hot-path workload concerns. If we ship hybrid pivot for fuel, then ship hybrid pivot for e-graph cost extraction, we've established the hybrid as the de facto template across multiple tracks.

### Exploration of alternatives

Drafted 5 alternative on-network optimization paths (A-E):
- A: Cell-as-Box with adaptive storage (cell API uniform; internal storage adapts to no-speculation hot path)
- B: Threshold-as-cell-property (derived value on read; eliminates propagator-as-decoration)
- C: Propagator-fire skip for monotone counters (cell declares `:fires-on '(threshold-crossing)`)
- D: Hot/warm/cold cells (extends BSP-LE Track 0's hot/cold split to cells)
- E: BSP-aware piggyback (threshold check in scheduler drain pass)

### User's architectural insight

User articulated the load-bearing principle: "Cell / Propagator / Scheduler Orthogonality" — the three layers must remain orthogonal. CALM theorem says monotone computation on fixed topology converges regardless of evaluation order — the order is the scheduler's concern; the computation is the cell+propagator's concern.

User specifically called out: "The problem I see, is about coupling too much on the scheduler, complecting what should be orthogonal — making a thin scheduler and portability more tricky."

This rejected Option E (couples to BSP scheduler; Gauss-Seidel and future Zig-LLVM schedulers have no equivalent drain pass; loses portability) and validated Options A+B+C+D (all scheduler-neutral; live at cell/propagator layer).

### Codification

Codified Cell/Propagator/Scheduler Orthogonality as a top-level principle in DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org. The principle establishes:
1. Three orthogonal layers (Cell, Propagator, Scheduler)
2. Optimization location rule (cell-level storage; propagator-level fire-pattern; scheduler-level efficiency only)
3. Anti-pattern: scheduler-coupled optimization (Option E was the example)
4. Portability implication (Prologos networks should be runnable across language boundaries — Racket prototype, Zig+LLVM lowering, future self-hosted runtime)
5. Multiple-scheduler invariance (Gauss-Seidel, BSP, Zig-LLVM all run same network with same semantics)
6. Distributed-concurrent runtime belongs at scheduler+networking layer, NOT propagator network itself

Cross-references added at:
- .claude/rules/propagator-design.md (propagator-level operational rule + anti-pattern catch)
- .claude/rules/on-network.md (on-network constraint extension — on-network is necessary but not sufficient; orthogonality determines the RIGHT LAYER)

Lesson codified at DEVELOPMENT_LESSONS.org with prophylactic discipline for future tracks (PReduce, OE, SH Series).

MASTER_ROADMAP.org § OE Series caveat refined from "hybrid-as-scaffolding-NOT-template" warning to "Cell/Propagator/Scheduler Orthogonality inheritance discipline."

### Hybrid pivot status — under reconsideration

D.3.EC findings about the hybrid pivot (P1, P2, P3, P4, P6 from D.2.SC) become MOOT under the on-network reframing:
- P1 (principle inversion) → moot (cell IS primary)
- P2 (belt-and-suspenders) → moot (no dual mechanism)
- P3 (cell staleness contract) → moot (cell IS live state)
- P4 (decomplection-vs-incomplete) → moot (no incomplete migration)
- P6 (hybrid-as-scaffolding-NOT-template) → moot (no hybrid to template)

The findings about multi-quantale composition (S3, S4), C-series gate (S5), Q-1C-3 (R3, CL2) remain relevant — they're about algebraic correctness, unaffected by the on-network reframing.

Issue #55 may need to be closed as "superseded by D.4 principled on-network design" depending on the redesign outcome.

### Next steps

1. ✅ Codify Cell/Propagator/Scheduler Orthogonality principle (this commit)
2. ⬜ Re-design Phase 1B as on-network optimized cell type (D.4) — likely 1-2 sessions
3. ⬜ Run new Pre-0 spike measuring optimized cell-write (~30-60 min)
4. ⬜ Revisit hybrid pivot decision with that data
5. ⬜ If on-network optimization succeeds: retire hybrid pivot scaffolding before it ships; D.4 commits on-network architecture as canonical
6. ⬜ If on-network optimization fails: hybrid pivot ships with stronger empirical grounding (direct cell-write measurement, not extrapolation)

### Lessons + observations (codification candidates)

- **User-articulated architectural principle in response to design pressure**: this is the second time this session a user-articulated insight reshaped the design (previously: "memory as first-class measurement axis" at Pre-0 planning). Pattern: when the design accumulates principle-violations, the user surfaces a higher-level principle that reframes the choice. Worth codifying: design pressure SURFACES architectural principles; the principles ARE the resolution to the pressure.

- **Codifying foundational principles immediately (not as watching-list candidates)**: the Cell/Propagator/Scheduler Orthogonality went straight to DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org top-level (not watching list). Rationale: foundational principles that prevent compounding architectural debt across multiple tracks should be codified immediately on identification, not deferred to watching-list-graduation thresholds. The cost of one codification session is much less than the cost of re-deriving the principle for each future track that needs it.

- **External critique surfacing previously-unconsidered architectural reframings**: D.3.EC was the first round to make external-perspective concerns explicit; the user's response to the critique introduced the orthogonality principle. External critique discipline pays off when the critique opens conceptual space the self-critique couldn't (because self-critique was operating within the existing design's assumptions).

### Surprises

- **The hybrid pivot's empirical foundation has a gap I didn't recognize until external critique surfaced it**: Pre-0 measured struct-copy + extrapolated to cell-write. The extrapolation was reasonable but UNVERIFIED for the specific cell mechanism we'd actually implement. D.3.EC MG1 surfaced this; the user's reframing made it load-bearing.

- **Option E (BSP-aware piggyback) was tempting because "easy to implement in current scheduler"** — exactly the anti-pattern the orthogonality principle catches. I proposed it; user rejected it on portability grounds. Lesson: when an optimization is appealing because of current-scheduler convenience, that's the moment to ask whether it'll survive scheduler changes.

- **PReduce series inheritance was the load-bearing argument**: the user's concern wasn't just principle-purity for its own sake — it was "PReduce will face the same hot-path workload, and shipping hybrid for fuel then again for e-graph cost would establish hybrid as the template across multiple tracks." Architectural debt has compound costs across multiple tracks; codifying the principle now prevents that compounding.

### Mempalace state

Unchanged.

🫡 Cell/Propagator/Scheduler Orthogonality codified. Hybrid pivot under reconsideration; D.4 redesign as on-network optimized cell type opens next session. PReduce + OE + SH Series inherit the orthogonality discipline prophylactically.

### Key insight: "the rare design state where two architectures are live simultaneously"

D.4 doesn't retract D.3 — it provides an ALTERNATIVE architecture. The Pre-0 spike acts as the falsification test. After the spike, exactly one architecture ships. This is the proper discipline when:
- The principle-aligned architecture (D.4) was previously extrapolated-but-not-measured
- The empirical fallback (D.3) is principle-violating but measurement-supported
- The alternative SHOULD be measured before committing to the fallback

The discipline applies prophylactically to future tracks: NEVER extrapolate a principle-violating commit when the alternative can be directly measured.

- §15 Parity test skeleton not yet updated for D.4

These complete in subsequent sessions after the §13.6 spike result is known. Doing them now would be speculative work that may need redo if the spike fails.

### Lessons + observations (codification candidates)

- **"Conditional commit state" with falsification test as the deciding factor**: this is the principled response when an architectural choice rests on extrapolation. Codify: when D.X commits a principle-violating architecture on EXTRAPOLATED measurement, the next round (D.X+1) should always include a falsification test that DIRECTLY measures the alternative before locking in. Watching list: 1 data point (this session); validate with future tracks.

- **D.4 finding-status table mapping all D.2.SC + D.3.EC findings to D.4 outcomes**: useful artifact. When a major architectural reframing happens, explicitly track which previous-round findings become MOOT vs UNCHANGED. Without this table, the previous-round critique work is left in design-doc limbo (closed under D.3 framing; ambiguous under D.4). Watching list: 1 data point.

- **Scaffolding pass as a distinct phase from full redesign**: doing the SCAFFOLDING (version bump + headline subsections + status notes on superseded sections) BEFORE the full content drill-down preserves design momentum without committing speculative work. If the spike falsifies D.4, the scaffolding annotates D.3 as canonical with minimal rework; if the spike validates D.4, the scaffolding is the foundation for the full revision. Watching list: 1 data point.

## 2026-05-14 (cont.) — §13.6 Pre-0 spike executed: ✓ PASS — D.4 canonical

Spike implementation + execution per design doc §13.6. Throwaway mock specialized cell mechanism in `racket/prologos/benchmarks/micro/bench-specialized-cell-spike.rkt` (~290 lines); ~5 min execution; results saved to `racket/prologos/data/benchmarks/tropical-spike-d4-2026-05-14.txt`.

### Results vs §13.6 targets

| Measurement | Target | Actual | Margin | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W1 specialized cell-write FAST PATH (avg n=1/100/10000) | ≤ 30 ns | **2.1–2.4 ns** | ~13× under | ✓ PASS |
| W1+ specialized cell-write WITH dispatch overhead (realistic) | ≤ 30 ns | **6.4–7.0 ns** | ~4× under | ✓ PASS |
| W2 specialized cell-write THRESHOLD CROSSING (rare) | ≤ 200 ns | **2.1–2.6 ns** | ~90× under | ✓ PASS |
| W3 GC time across 5×100k decrements | ZERO major-GC | **0.000 ms (0.0%)** | structural | ✓ PASS |
| W3 alloc / retain (10×100k decrements) | (reference) | **1.1 KB / ~0.0 KB** | vs A7.3 6251 KB / -0.3 KB | dramatic improvement |
| W4 specialized cell-read | ≤ 15 ns | **0.8–0.9 ns** | ~17× under | ✓ PASS |
| W1+ + W4 per-decrement cycle (realistic) | ≤ 45 ns | **7.3 ns** | ~6× under | ✓ PASS |

### Decision: ✓ PASS — D.4 canonical

Per §13.6 decision criteria:
- ✓ W1+ ≤ 30 ns (6.4 ns; with realistic dispatch overhead)
- ✓ W3 ZERO major-GC (0.000 ms across 5×100k = 500k operations)
- ✓ W4 ≤ 15 ns (0.8 ns)
- ✓ (W1+W4) ≤ 45 ns (7.3 ns realistic cycle)

**Conclusion**: the specialized cell type framework (§4.6 NTT model — `:tier 'hot` + `:storage 'monotone-counter` + `:fires-on 'threshold-crossing` + `:on-write-check`) is feasible. The on-network architecture (D.4) provides:
- Per-decrement cell-write at ~6.4 ns (with dispatch) — far below struct-copy baseline (24 ns from Pre-0 M7) and the 30 ns target
- ZERO major-GC pressure at 100k decrements — structurally guaranteed by direct fixnum mutation under no-speculation
- Allocation profile of ~1.1 KB per 100k operations (vs struct-copy's 6251 KB per A7.3) — **5700× improvement** on memory pressure

The D.3 hybrid pivot's empirical motivation (Pre-0 R-19 extrapolation: "full cell-migration would trigger major GC") is **falsified for the specialized cell type framework**. The hybrid pivot was justified by an extrapolation that doesn't hold once the specialized storage strategy is in place.

### Spike methodology refinements applied mid-run

Initial measurement showed W1 ≈ 2 ns/call, raising a concern about JIT dead-code elimination. Two refinements applied for honesty:

1. **Added W1+ measurement with dispatch overhead** (~290 lines total file): cell-meta lookup via `vector-ref` + tier check + under-speculation check before entering the fast path. This represents the realistic upper bound for the production implementation. Result: 6.4–7.0 ns/call — still well within target.

2. **Defeated dead-code elimination in baseline (B.M7)**: captured `struct-copy` results in mutable boxes; added a nested struct-copy variant mirroring the real M7 pattern (`prop-network → prop-net-hot`). Result: B.M7.2 = 5.7–5.9 ns/call (vs Pre-0 M7's 24 ns/call). The gap is explained by simpler mock structs (mock-network has 3 fields vs real prop-network's 3 fields wrapping prop-net-hot with multiple fields) — not by elimination. The W1 measurement of 2.1 ns is robust (verified via W2 crossing-count: exactly 50100 = 50000 measurement + 100 warmup, proving the mutation IS happening).

### Critical observations

1. **The W3 zero-GC result is structurally robust**: direct fixnum mutation on a struct's mutable field allocates ZERO bytes. The 1.1 KB allocation observed across 100k operations is from the loop machinery (`in-range` iterators, etc.), not from the cell-write itself. This eliminates the entire empirical foundation of the D.3 hybrid pivot.

2. **The realistic upper bound includes idiomatic dispatch**: my W1+ mock uses `vector-ref` for cell-meta lookup (1 ns) + 3 `eq?` checks for dispatch (1-2 ns) + the fast path mutation (~2 ns) + call overhead. Real implementation would use a similar vector-indexed structure. 6.4 ns is realistic, not optimistic.

3. **The framework's PReduce/OE inheritance is now empirically grounded**: future tracks targeting `:tier 'hot` + `:storage 'monotone-counter` cells inherit a fast-path with measured performance characteristics. The §4.6 framework is no longer speculative.

### Verdict consequences (per §13.6 + handoff §7.1 PASS branch)

D.4 becomes canonical. The next actions are:

1. **Complete D.4 §9 Phase 1B full revision**: specialized cell framework implementation plan; cell-meta fields; dispatch logic; registration API; tests (~250-450 LoC scope)
2. **Complete D.4 §10 Phase 1C full revision**: direct migration patterns; 6 sub-phases (1C-i through 1C-vi); D-1C-7/8/9 drift risks (~150-250 LoC scope)
3. **Update D.4 §15 parity test skeleton** for D.4 (drop hybrid-specific staleness contract tests; add specialized cell semantics tests)
4. **Update Pre-0 plan separate doc** with §13.6 cross-reference + the spike result
5. **Close GitHub Issue #55** as "superseded by D.4 principled on-network design" (with link to spike result + dailies)
6. **Retire DEFERRED.md "PPN 4C tropical addendum: hybrid pivot scaffolding retirement" entry** (no scaffolding to track)
7. **Mark D.3 hybrid-specific sections as PERMANENTLY SUPERSEDED** (status updates from SUPERSEDED-BY-D.4 to RETIRED-PER-D.4-CANONICAL)
8. **Update MASTER_ROADMAP.org § OE Series caveat** — orthogonality inheritance discipline preserved; remove hybrid-pivot-as-scaffolding language (no scaffolding exists)
9. **Commit D.4 canonical state**

This is substantial scope (~5-8 file edits, ~2-3 commits) and likely exceeds the ~1h conversational cadence. Will pause to discuss scope/sequence with user before proceeding.

### Codification candidates (refined from this session)

- **"Conditional commit state with falsification test" — promoted from watching-list to STRONG candidate**: 1 full data point now executed end-to-end. The pattern: when D.X commits a principle-violating architecture on extrapolated measurement, D.X+1 should always include a falsification test that DIRECTLY measures the alternative before locking in. Validated this session — the spike took ~30 min and decisively resolved the architecture choice. Estimate: 1 more data point in future tracks → graduation.

- **"Dispatch overhead in spike measurements should be explicit"**: the initial spike measurement (~2 ns/call) was suspicious because it skipped dispatch overhead. Adding the W1+ "realistic upper bound" measurement (with vector-ref lookup + dispatch checks) gave a defensible verdict. Pattern: when a spike measures a fast path, include a measurement that mirrors the production dispatch overhead — otherwise the spike's verdict is over-optimistic. Watching list: 1 data point.

- **"Baseline stability check requires defeating dead-code elimination"**: my initial B.M7 stability check measured 0.8 ns vs Pre-0 M7's 24 ns — a 30× gap explained by JIT eliminating the unused struct-copy result. Fix: capture result in a mutable box (`set-box!`). Pattern: any baseline stability check that doesn't observe its result will be silently dead-code-eliminated; always capture. Watching list: 1 data point.

## 2026-05-15 — Phase 1B mini-design: friend's question surfaces Option 13 deferred-write refinement + per-phase measurement discipline

User opened Phase 1B mini-design+audit ("What's at play, what does 1B want to be, what design questions are open, what does this forward-enable?"). I structured a mini-design walkthrough with 9 design questions (Q-A through Q-I) covering cell-meta storage representation, module organization, dispatch overhead, storage strategy/fire-on enum scope, predicate API timing, and speculation interaction. Q-A (cell-meta storage) was identified as most architecturally consequential.

### The friend's first question: "What writes the fuel cell — scheduler, fire function, or topology?"

Sharp question. Verified in code that fuel decrements happen at exactly 4 sites in `propagator.rkt` (lines 1852, 1887, 3000, 3053) — ALL inside BSP scheduler fire-loop code, none in propagator fire functions. Under D.4, these become `(net-cell-write net fuel-cost-cell-id ...)`. The writer is the SCHEDULER.

This surfaced an architectural category that the D.4 design implicitly assumed but didn't name: **scheduler-state cells** (cells the scheduler writes; distinct from propagator-state and topology-state). The D.4 design didn't violate orthogonality at the cell BEHAVIOR layer (cell semantics are scheduler-neutral) but had implicit scheduler-cell coupling at the WRITE-SITE layer (scheduler references cell-id 11). This coupling is appropriate because fuel IS a scheduler concern.

Drafted Q-J + drift risk to surface this in the design doc. User asked to defer codifying as a principle until we explore tradeoffs more carefully.

### The friend's second question: "scheduler has to track and treat differently a fuelcell network... it'll be slower than implementing a gas tracker natively"

Sharp question #2. Did the honest performance analysis:

| Approach | Per-cycle cost | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Current native struct-copy (combined with worklist update) | ~24 ns | Pre-0 M7 |
| Pure native (set-box! on private box) | ~2-4 ns | Used at lines 1852/1887 |
| D.4 specialized cell-write with realistic dispatch | ~6 ns + ~22 ns struct-copy for worklist = ~28 ns | §13.6 spike W1+ |

D.4 as designed: ~4 ns/cycle overhead vs current; ~3-4 ns vs theoretical native. Across 100k decrements: ~0.13% wall-time overhead. Within budget per spike's PASS verdict, but NOT optimal.

Presented three framings: Option X (ship framework, leave fuel native); Option Y (D.4 as designed); Option Z (pause and revisit). User asked: "Is there some other approach that we haven't considered that would bring us closer to the theoretical minimum?"

### Option 13 — Scheduler-side deferred write at round boundaries

The architectural lever the friend's question made visible: **D.4 assumed `net-cell-write` should be called per-fire**. The orthogonality principle does NOT require this — only that the cell's BEHAVIOR is scheduler-neutral. The scheduler is free to CHOOSE WHEN to call `net-cell-write`.

The pattern:
- BSP scheduler reads fuel-cost cell at round entry → local-var (box)
- Per fire: inline local-var decrement + threshold check (~2 ns)
- BSP scheduler writes cell at round exit OR immediately on exhaustion
- On-write check runs at flush time (which is round boundary for normal flow)

Performance estimated at ~2 ns/cycle amortized — matches native gas tracker; ~3× better than D.4 per-fire pattern. **Pending §13.6.A spike validation**.

### Why Option 13 is materially different from D.3 hybrid pivot (key insight)

D.3 hybrid: struct field was PRIMARY (live state); cell was DERIVED (lazy sync). Principle INVERSION.

Option 13: cell IS PRIMARY (canonical); local-var is scheduler-internal SCRATCH (invisible outside BSP fire loop). NO principle inversion.

The cell IS canonical at every observation point. The local-var is just the scheduler's working register; mid-round staleness is unobservable (Phase 3C consumers fire AT round boundaries, where the cell is current).

This is the architecturally cleanest answer to the friend's concern. It preserves all D.4 benefits (cell observability, framework reuse, scheduler portability) while achieving near-native performance.

### User direction: "Important refinement; let's capture this; also measure throughout to validate assumptions"

User confirmed Option 13 as an important refinement to capture, AND added a discipline ask: measurement gates throughout Stage 4 implementation to validate assumptions, with A/B/C testing structure to compare approaches.

### Design doc updates in this commit

**Option 13 capture**:
- §10.3.A NEW: "Option 13 — Scheduler-side deferred write at round boundaries" (~110 lines) — full pattern, performance characterization, A/B/C comparison vs D.3 hybrid + D.4 per-fire, orthogonality preservation argument, scheduler-state cell category confirmation, trade-offs + complications, optional Option 14 macro-specialization
- §10.4 sub-phase plan: REFINED for Option 13 — 1C-i runs §13.6.A spike; 1C-ii migrates to deferred-write pattern; 1C-iv has macro-specialization decision point; 1C-vi has full A/B/C report
- §11.3 Phase 1V exit criteria: D.3 hybrid pivot gates RETIRED-PER-D.4-CANONICAL; replaced with Option 13 deferred-write performance gate + architectural gate + cell observability gate + speculation correctness gate + Option 14 decision gate

**§13.6.A NEW — Option 13 spike plan**: ~80 lines. W1-O13 through W4-O13 measurements; A/B/C comparison structure; decision criteria (PASS → Option 13 canonical for 1C; FAIL → fall back to D.4 per-fire; MIXED → investigate); risk register (RO13-1 through RO13-4).

**§13.7 NEW — Per-Phase Measurement Plan** (D.4 CANONICAL discipline; ~80 lines):
- A/B/C structure for fuel-cost write pattern (A = current struct-copy / B = D.4 per-fire / C = Option 13 deferred-write)
- Per-sub-phase measurement gates across Phase 1B (1B-i through 1B-iv) and Phase 1C (1C-i through 1C-vi)
- For each sub-phase: WHAT is measured, AGAINST what target, with WHICH decision rule
- Cross-track measurement infrastructure note (bench-ab.rkt; possible --refs extension for A/B/C; suite-level S-tier integration)
- Honest framing of measurement overhead (~3-6 hours total across Stage 4; worth it because catches regressions early)

**Top-of-doc Revision Summary + §17 What's next**: updated to reflect Option 13 refinement + measurement discipline + §13.6.A pending; trajectory updated with 14 steps (was 12).

**DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org § Orthogonality**: NEW subsection "Scheduler-State Cells (refined 2026-05-15)". Names the three cell categories (propagator-state, scheduler-state, topology-state); explains why scheduler-state cells permit deferred-write optimization that other cells don't; canonical example via fuel-cost cell + Option 13 pattern; cross-references to §13.6.A + §13.7.

**DEVELOPMENT_LESSONS.org**: two NEW codification candidates:
1. **"Spike Validates Feasibility, Not Optimality"** — a passing Pre-0 spike doesn't close design exploration; refinements within the architecture are still valuable post-spike (1 data point: Option 13 discovered post-§13.6 spike PASS).
2. **"Per-Phase Measurement Plan with A/B/C Structure"** — when design correctness rests on performance assumptions, the implementation plan should EXPLICITLY name measurements at each sub-phase boundary with A/B/C variants (1 data point: §13.7 for PPN 4C).

### Key architectural observations

1. **The friend's question revealed an architectural lever the spike didn't test**. The §13.6 spike validated "D.4 per-fire is feasible" — it did NOT validate "per-fire is optimal vs deferred-write." Option 13 emerged from outside-perspective exploration AFTER the spike's PASS verdict.

2. **Scheduler-state cells are a clean architectural category**. Naming this category explicitly (in DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org) clarifies what's appropriate and what's not. Scheduler-state cells permit scheduler-side write-batching; propagator-state cells do not.

3. **The deferred-write pattern is generalizable**. Future scheduler-state cells (BSP worklist-cache cell already exists; future BSP-round-counter cell; etc.) can apply the same pattern. Each scheduler chooses its own deferred-write strategy suited to its loop structure.

4. **Measurement discipline as design artifact**. The §13.7 per-phase measurement plan is a NEW kind of design-doc section — explicitly capturing "what we'll measure and against what targets" rather than relying on Stage 4's general "per-phase microbench" discipline. This is stronger because it names CONCRETE comparisons (A/B/C) and DECISION RULES.

### Lessons + observations (refined from this conversation)

- **External-question-driven design exploration**: the friend's question surfaced an architectural lever that the spike's framing missed. Pattern: outside-perspective questions DURING Phase mini-design are especially valuable AFTER a Pre-0 spike PASS (the spike provides data; the question reframes the architectural envelope). Watching list: 2 data points (user surfaced orthogonality principle from D.3.EC + friend surfaced Option 13 from Phase 1B mini-design); ~1 more for codification of "External questions surface architectural levers internal exploration misses."

- **The Q-J → Option 13 progression validates "Spike Validates Feasibility, Not Optimality"**: the spike answered "can D.4 work" but didn't answer "is D.4 the best design within the architecture's envelope." The codification candidate above captures this discipline.

- **A/B/C measurement discipline turns Stage 4 from "trust the design" to "validate the design"**: per-sub-phase gates with concrete decision rules transform implementation from execution-of-design to ongoing-validation-of-design. The measurement work is real overhead but catches regressions when they're cheap.

### Mempalace state

Unchanged.

🫡 Phase 1B mini-design + Option 13 refinement complete. The design now includes (a) the deferred-write pattern that achieves near-native performance while preserving all D.4 architectural benefits; (b) §13.6.A spike plan to VALIDATE the ~2 ns/cycle amortized estimate before locking in; (c) §13.7 Per-Phase Measurement Plan that builds A/B/C validation gates throughout Stage 4. The friend's questions about fuel-cell writes + native-vs-cell-API performance surfaced both a clean architectural category (scheduler-state cells) AND a refinement to D.4 (Option 13). DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org § Orthogonality refined with the new category; DEVELOPMENT_LESSONS.org has two new codification candidates. Next: Phase 1C-i mini-audit executes §13.6.A spike; if it PASSES, Phase 1C-ii migrates BSP fire sites to Option 13 deferred-write pattern.

## 2026-05-15 (cont.) — §13.6.A Option 13 spike executed: ✓ PASS — Option 13 canonical, beats native by 2.4×

Per user direction "1" — proceed with spike execution. Extended `bench-specialized-cell-spike.rkt` with §13.6.A W1-O13 through W4-O13 measurements (~280 LoC addition); ~3 min execution; results saved to `racket/prologos/data/benchmarks/tropical-spike-d4-option13-2026-05-15.txt`.

### Spike results vs §13.6.A targets

| Measurement | Target | Result | Margin |
|---|---|---|---|
| W1-O13a function-call per-fire | ≤ 5 ns | **2.2 ns/call** | ~2.3× under |
| W1-O13b macro-inline per-fire | ≤ 5 ns | **2.1 ns/call** | ~2.4× under |
| W2a-O13 round-entry cell-read | ≤ 15 ns | **1.3 ns/call** | ~11.5× under |
| W2b-O13 round-exit cell-write | ≤ 15 ns | **1.4 ns/call** | ~10.7× under |
| W3-O13a function-call amortized N=100 | ≤ 3 ns | **2.16 ns/cycle** | ~1.4× under |
| W3-O13b macro-inline amortized N=100 | ≤ 3 ns | **2.14 ns/cycle** | ~1.4× under |
| W4-O13 exhaustion flush + contradiction | ≤ 50 ns | **3.7 ns/call** | ~13.5× under |

All 7 targets PASS with margins ranging from 1.4× to 13.5× under.

### A/B/C comparison (the architecturally-significant result)

| Variant | Per-cycle cost | vs Option 13 |
|---|---|---|
| A — Current native struct-copy (B.M7.2 in spike) | 5.2 ns/cycle | Option 13 is **2.4× faster** |
| B — D.4 per-fire cell-write w/ dispatch (W1+ from §13.6) | 6.6 ns/cycle | Option 13 is **3.1× faster** |
| C-fn — Option 13 function-call (CANONICAL) | **2.16 ns/cycle** | — |
| C-macro — Option 13 + Option 14 macro | 2.14 ns/cycle | savings 0.02 ns/cycle vs C-fn |

**Option 14 macro specialization decision (per §10.4 1C-iv gate)**: savings 0.02 ns/cycle, far below the 1 ns threshold. **SKIP Option 14** — function-call variant achieves the target on its own.

### The key insight from the spike

The spike's data falsified one of its own design assumptions. §10.3.A originally framed Option 13 as "approximating native gas tracker performance" — implying it would match native, not exceed it. But the measurement shows Option 13 is **structurally FASTER** than current native struct-copy:
- Current native: nested `(struct-copy prop-network ...)` + `(struct-copy prop-net-hot ...)` per BSP cycle, allocating new structs per cycle (~5.2 ns including the struct allocation cost)
- Option 13: mutable-box decrement (no allocation) per cycle, with round-boundary cell-write (~6 ns once per 100s-1000s fires) — amortized to ~2.2 ns/cycle

The deferred-write pattern's mutable-box approach is structurally cheaper than struct-copy because it avoids per-cycle allocation. This is a 2.4× perf improvement vs the CURRENT implementation, not just "no regression vs native" as I had assumed.

### Why this validates the per-phase measurement discipline (§13.7)

The user's discipline ask ("measure throughout to validate assumptions") is now empirically vindicated. Without the §13.6.A spike, the design would have shipped with:
- An ASSUMPTION that Option 13 is "approximately native" (~5 ns/cycle, matching B.M7.2)
- A reality where Option 13 is structurally BETTER than native (~2 ns/cycle, beating native by 2.4×)

Without measurement, we'd have committed to a design while being wrong about its actual performance characteristics. The spike took ~10 min to extend + 3 min to run, and yielded data that the design rewrite (§10.3.A + top-of-doc + §17) now references as MEASURED rather than ESTIMATED.

Similarly, without the spike, we wouldn't have known to SKIP Option 14 macro specialization. Spike showed macro savings 0.02 ns/cycle — far below the 1 ns threshold. The Option 14 decision is now data-driven, not speculation-driven.

### Design doc updates in this commit

- **§13.6.A**: full spike result tables added (status: VALIDATED 2026-05-15); Option 14 SKIP decision recorded
- **§10.3.A**: performance characterization table updated from "ESTIMATED" to "MEASURED" with actual ns/cycle numbers; cell-as-FASTER-than-native insight captured
- **Top-of-doc Revision Summary**: D.4 REFINEMENT marked ✓ VALIDATED; spike results table embedded; "spike falsified its own design assumption" insight named
- **§17 What's next + Document status**: trajectory steps 12-13 marked ✅ (was 🔄 ⬜); step 14 (Stage 4 implementation) remains pending

### Lessons + observations

- **The §13.6.A spike result validates "Spike Validates Feasibility, Not Optimality"** (DEVELOPMENT_LESSONS.org codification candidate from this session arc). The §13.6 spike validated D.4 per-fire as FEASIBLE; the §13.6.A spike validated Option 13 deferred-write as not just feasible, but materially better than the spike-PASSED alternative. Refinement-after-spike-PASS produced a 3× improvement.

- **The Option 14 decision is the canonical "measurement-driven decision" case**: I drafted Option 14 as a possible refinement; measured its actual benefit (0.02 ns/cycle); used the data to SKIP. Without measurement, "macro specialization usually helps" intuition would have argued for shipping it. Empirical data showed: not in this case. This is what §13.7's per-phase measurement gates do across Stage 4.

- **"Faster than native" outcomes for on-network refactorings**: Option 13 beats native struct-copy because struct-copy has per-cycle allocation overhead that mutable-box doesn't. Pattern: when an existing native implementation uses persistent data structures with high allocation costs, an on-network refactoring that uses scheduler-private mutable state can beat the native. This is counter-intuitive — "on-network = slower than native" is a common assumption that's not always true. Watching list: 1 data point (this spike); validate with future tracks.

### Codification candidates updated

- **"Spike Validates Feasibility, Not Optimality"** (added previous commit): now has its first VALIDATED data point — Option 13 was found post-§13.6 spike PASS and proved better than spike-PASSED alternative. Strengthens the watching-list status.

- **"Measurement-driven decision-making catches assumption-vs-reality gaps"** (NEW from this spike): a design's stated targets are HYPOTHESES until measured. §10.3.A's original "approximating native" framing was wrong — Option 13 is 2.4× faster than native. Pattern: when a design assumption can be measured cheaply, measure it; don't ship the assumption as fact. Watching list: 1 data point.

### Mempalace state

Unchanged.

🫡 §13.6.A Option 13 spike PASSED dramatically. The deferred-write pattern at round boundaries achieves **2.16 ns/cycle amortized** — **2.4× faster than current native struct-copy** and **3.1× faster than D.4 per-fire pattern**. Option 14 macro specialization SKIPPED based on data (savings 0.02 ns/cycle, below threshold). Stage 4 implementation is ready: Phase 1B substrate first, Phase 1C migrates BSP fire sites to Option 13 with §13.7 measurement gates at each sub-phase boundary. The discipline of measuring vs. assuming has paid off twice in this session arc — the §13.6 spike (D.4 canonical) and now §13.6.A spike (Option 13 canonical + Option 14 SKIP).

## 2026-05-15 (cont.) — Phase 1B mini-design Q-A through Q-N walkthrough + audit correction: parallel BSP is production default; §10.3.A two-variant rewrite

User direction: walk through Phase 1B mini-design open questions Q-A through Q-N with the Option 13 + §13.6.A spike + §13.7 measurement discipline applied. Resolve where possible; defer to code where appropriate. Persist resolutions back into the design doc.

### User challenge on Q-M (parallel BSP composition)

I had marked Q-M (parallel BSP composition) as "deferred to Phase 2 PAR." User challenged: "Our current BSP scheduler IS the parallel BSP from PAR research (PAR essentially closed for now). Is that not so? This should be the design we're building against. Can you audit carefully to verify?"

### Audit findings (verified in code)

**The user was right.** `driver.rkt:435` sets `current-parallel-executor` globally to `(make-parallel-thread-fire-all)`. The production scheduler IS parallel BSP. PAR Track 2 R1-R2 closed with BSP-as-production-default and parallel execution proven (380/380 GREEN, 4.45× speedup at scale).

Further audit revealed **FIVE scheduler entry points**, each with its own fuel-decrement pattern:

| # | Function (line) | Loop structure | Current decrement pattern |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `run-to-quiescence-inner` (1835-1866) | Sequential Gauss-Seidel | `set-box!` per-fire |
| 2 | `run-to-quiescence-inner/traced` (1870-1898) | Same + tracing | `set-box!` per-fire |
| 3 | `run-to-quiescence-bsp` (2315+; **production default**) | **Parallel BSP** | `struct-copy [fuel (- ... n)]` at line 2384 — **batch by N at round entry, ALREADY** |
| 4 | `run-widen-phase` (2989+) | Sequential widening | `struct-copy [fuel (sub1 ...)]` per-fire |
| 5 | `run-narrow-phase` (3042+) | Sequential narrowing | `struct-copy [fuel (sub1 ...)]` per-fire |

The CRITICAL discovery: **the production parallel BSP main loop (#3) ALREADY does batch-decrement-by-N at round entry**. It's not per-fire. So fuel decrements happen ON THE MAIN THREAD (sequentially), BEFORE workers spin up. Workers fire against the post-decrement snapshot but don't touch fuel.

### Architectural correction to §10.3.A

The original §10.3.A pseudocode described a single per-fire local-var pattern. But the audit shows this pattern is WRONG for the parallel BSP main loop (which has no per-fire body). It's CORRECT for the sequential schedulers (#1, #2, #4, #5).

**Two-variant rewrite**:
- **Variant A — Round-entry batch decrement**: parallel BSP main loop. ONE `net-cell-write` per BSP round, on main thread, before workers. Cost: ~6 ns/round; amortized ~0.06 ns/cycle at N=100.
- **Variant B — Local-var + cell-write at phase boundaries**: four sequential schedulers. Local-var box + per-fire decrement + cell-write at phase end. Cost: ~2.16 ns/cycle amortized (per §13.6.A spike).

The scheduler CHOOSES the variant fitting its loop structure. Both variants preserve orthogonality (cell mechanism is unchanged; scheduler's choice of when to invoke `net-cell-write` is its own concern).

### Walkthrough of Phase 1B mini-design questions with Option 13 + audit applied

Most architectural questions can now be RESOLVED. Implementation-detail questions remain deferred to per-phase mini-design+audit with code in hand.

**Resolved questions (architectural; 7 of 8)**:
- Q-1B-8 → **A2** (cell-meta on prop-cell struct itself). Performance pressure dissolved under Option 13 (dispatch fires at boundaries, not per fire). A2 wins on cleanliness.
- Q-1B-9 → **F2** (predicate receives `(current, new-value, net)`). Forward-compat for "threshold crossed this write" semantics.
- Q-1B-10 → **B1-prime** (cell-meta + registration + dispatch in `propagator.rkt`; thin `specialized-cells.rkt` for convenience constructors; BSP deferred-write helpers in BSP scheduler code).
- Q-1B-11 → **D1** (storage strategy enum: just `'general` + `'monotone-counter`).
- Q-1B-12 → **E1** (fire-on policy enum: just `'any-change` + `'threshold-crossing`).
- Q-1B-13 → **G1** (predicate runs AFTER merge; sees the cell-state-after-write).
- Q-1B-14 → **L1** (local-var = let-scoped `box`, ephemeral per scheduler-phase invocation).

**Deferred to code (3)**: Q-1B-1 (API naming); Q-1B-2 (`+inf.0` vs sentinel); Q-1B-4 (residuation as helper vs propagator).

**Spike-validated (1)**: Q-1B-6 → ✓ PASS at §13.6.

**New questions from Option 13 + audit**:
- Q-1C-K → defer-to-1C-i-mini-audit (local-var flush timing enumeration).
- Q-1C-L → resolved L1 per Q-1B-14.
- Q-1C-M → **RESOLVED IN-SCOPE** (was DEFER-TO-PAR-FUTURE; corrected per audit). Parallel BSP composition under Variant A works because main thread serializes fuel-state updates; workers don't touch fuel.
- Q-1C-N → forward-capture in §13.7 (other scheduler-state cells using deferred-write pattern).

### Design doc updates in this commit

**§10.3.A — corrected and rewritten (two-variant pattern)**:
- Scheduler entry points + variant assignment matrix (5 entries; #1-#5 with Variant A or B)
- Variant A pseudocode (round-entry batch; ~6 ns/round; ~0.06 ns/cycle amortized)
- Variant B pseudocode (local-var per-fire; ~2.16 ns/cycle amortized; matches original §10.3.A pattern)
- Performance characterization expanded with both variants' costs
- Trade-offs: "Parallel BSP" changed from "deferred to Phase 2 PAR" to "RESOLVED IN-SCOPE" with explanation
- Spike-falsified-its-own-assumption note updated to mention both variants

**§10.4 sub-phase plan**:
- 1C-i mini-audit explicitly enumerates all 5 scheduler entry points with variant assignment
- 1C-ii migration applies the CORRECT variant per scheduler (Variant A for entry point #3; Variant B for #1, #2, #4, #5)
- 1C-iv records ✅ Option 14 SKIP decision (resolved at §13.6.A spike)

**§10.5 drift risks**:
- D-1C-10 NEW: applying wrong variant to wrong scheduler (e.g., Variant B per-fire to parallel BSP main loop would be wasteful + incorrect)
- D-1C-11 NEW: parallel BSP main loop's batch semantic must NOT be replaced with per-fire pattern

**§10.7 open questions**:
- Q-1C-K NEW: local-var flush timing (defer to 1C-i mini-audit)
- Q-1C-L resolved L1
- Q-1C-M RESOLVED IN-SCOPE (corrected from DEFER-TO-PAR-FUTURE)
- Q-1C-N forward-captured in §13.7

**§9.9 open questions**:
- Reorganized: implementation-detail questions vs architectural questions vs spike-validated
- 7 questions RESOLVED with specific leans (Q-1B-8 → A2; Q-1B-9 → F2; Q-1B-10/11/12/13/14 added with resolutions)
- 3 questions remain deferred to code (Q-1B-1/2/4)
- Phase 1B mini-design status: 8 resolved + 3 deferred = ready for Stage 4

**Top-of-doc Revision Summary**:
- Added "Audit correction 2026-05-15" note explaining the parallel BSP discovery + two-variant rewrite
- Updated the spike-falsified-its-own-assumption framing to include Variant A's even-lower cost

**DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org § Scheduler-State Cells**:
- "The deferred-write pattern" subsection rewritten with TWO variants (A + B)
- Scheduler/variant assignment table (parallel BSP → A; Gauss-Seidel + widening + narrowing + sequential BSP → B)
- Both variants justified for orthogonality (cell behavior unchanged; scheduler chooses when to call `net-cell-write`)

### Key insights from this audit-correction work

1. **The "deferred to Phase 2 PAR" framing was lazy**. PAR Track 2 R1-R2 closed; parallel BSP IS production. Phase 1B/1C must handle parallel composition NOW, not defer it. User's challenge surfaced this gap.

2. **The audit revealed that the current production code is ALREADY closer to Option 13 than I thought**. The main BSP loop ALREADY does batch decrement at round entry (line 2384). Migrating to D.4 + Variant A is straightforward: change the struct-copy field update to a cell-write at the same site. The substrate changes; the concurrency pattern doesn't.

3. **One pattern doesn't fit all schedulers**. Five entry points with different loop structures need different variants. The original §10.3.A overgeneralized to "the BSP pattern" — but there are FIVE schedulers, each with its own loop. Variant A fits parallel BSP main; Variant B fits sequential schedulers.

4. **The §13.7 measurement discipline confirms its value**: without the spike + per-phase measurement plan, the original §10.3.A pseudocode (Variant B applied to all schedulers) might have shipped. The discipline catches gaps between design assumptions and code reality.

5. **External-question-driven exploration continues to pay off**. The friend's first question surfaced Option 13; the user's "is parallel BSP production?" question surfaced the audit gap + two-variant rewrite. Pattern: outside-perspective questions DURING mini-design surface architectural assumptions internal exploration misses.

### Lessons + observations

- **"Don't defer architectural questions that production has already resolved"** (codification candidate): when designing for a future architecture, verify whether that architecture is already production. If it is, the design must address it NOW, not defer. The "parallel BSP composition" question was already answered by PAR Track 2 R1-R2; my "defer to PAR future" framing missed that. Watching list: 1 data point.

- **"Audit the production scheduler when designing scheduler-state cells"** (codification candidate): scheduler-state cells interact with the scheduler's loop structure. Different schedulers have different loop structures; different variants of deferred-write apply. Don't assume one pattern fits all. The audit should enumerate ALL scheduler entry points + identify per-scheduler variant assignment. Watching list: 1 data point.

- **"The original pseudocode overgeneralized"** observation: §10.3.A described one pattern; reality has five entry points with two patterns. Origin: I conflated "BSP scheduler" with "all schedulers"; the codebase has Gauss-Seidel + widening + narrowing + sequential BSP + parallel BSP, all distinct. Pattern: when describing scheduler-side patterns, the design should explicitly enumerate WHICH schedulers the pattern applies to.

### Mempalace state

Unchanged.

🫡 Phase 1B mini-design walkthrough complete with audit correction. The design doc now distinguishes Variant A (parallel BSP main; ~0.06 ns/cycle amortized) from Variant B (sequential schedulers; ~2.16 ns/cycle amortized). All five scheduler entry points have explicit variant assignments. Q-M (parallel BSP composition) was incorrectly deferred earlier; now RESOLVED IN-SCOPE per the production scheduler audit. 7 of 8 architectural questions RESOLVED; 3 implementation-detail questions deferred to code. Phase 1B is ready to enter Stage 4 implementation with the design decisions locked in and validated empirically (Option 13 spike + Option 14 SKIP).

## 2026-05-15 (cont.) — Stage 4 Phase 1B-i mini-audit + cell-meta storage gate: ✓ PASS (4.06 ns/call)

Entering Stage 4 implementation per Per-Phase Protocol. Sub-phase ordering confirmed: single sessions in series (Order A). 1B-i is the first sub-phase — validates Q-1B-8 resolution A2 (meta field on `prop-cell` struct itself) via microbench before any production code change.

### Pre-1B-i discussion (with user)

Sketched 4-sub-phase structure (~400-700 LoC across ~30 min to ~1h each):
- 1B-i mini-audit + cell-meta storage gate (small; this commit)
- 1B-ii framework module + `net-cell-write` dispatch extension (medium)
- 1B-iii tropical-fuel module + SRE registration + C-series (medium)
- 1B-iv cell registration via framework + tests + close (small)

Three flavors for 1B-i microbench discussed: pure prototype (no production change), real struct field addition + microbench, or hybrid (prototype-only for gate; production change deferred to 1B-ii). User chose hybrid — cleanest phase boundary. Microbench location confirmed: `bench-ppn-track4c.rkt` (existing) per §13.7's mention.

### What was done

1. **Mini-audit codebase**: read `propagator.rkt:253` to confirm `prop-cell` struct shape `(value dependents)`. Found ~30 construction sites that would need updating to 3-arg under A2. Confirmed `struct-copy` sites auto-preserve fields. Verified cell-ids 11/12 (fuel-cost-cell + fuel-budget-cell per §4.3) are uncontested — `make-prop-network` (lines 621-660) allocates 11 well-known cells (cell-ids 0-10); 11/12 are the next slots.

2. **Wrote CM1-CM5 microbench section** in `bench-ppn-track4c.rkt` (inserted before the SUMMARY block; ~110 LoC). Methodology: mock structs mirror A2's layout (3-field `mock-cell-v2` vs 2-field `mock-cell-v1` baseline); DCE defeated via mutable-box accumulator (per §13.6 spike lesson). Measurements: construction cost (CM1), meta accessor (CM2 — the gate), backward-compat branch (CM3), chained tier access (CM4), full fast-path dispatch (CM5 with mock `mock-current-speculation` parameter mirroring production's `current-speculation-assumption`).

3. **Ran microbench**: 50000 iterations per measurement. Two printf format-string bugs caught + fixed (`~6` and `~50` were being parsed as format directives). Final clean run exit code 0.

4. **Saved results** to `racket/prologos/data/benchmarks/tropical-1b-i-cell-meta-2026-05-15.txt` (1.6 KB).

5. **Persisted findings** into design doc as new **§9.2.0 1B-i mini-audit findings** (~70 lines) covering codebase audit, microbench result table, gate verdict, and a surfaced 1B-ii architectural finding (see below).

6. **Updated Progress Tracker** in §3: split 1B row into 1B-i (✅ ✓ PASS) + 1B-ii / 1B-iii / 1B-iv (⬜).

### Results

| Measurement | Result | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| CM1.1 2-field construct (current baseline) | 5 ns/call | Current production rep |
| CM1.2 3-field construct, meta=#f (backward-compat) | 5 ns/call | **No construction regression for regular cells** |
| CM1.3 3-field construct, meta=sample (specialized) | 5 ns/call | No regression for specialized cells either |
| **CM2.2 meta accessor (CORE GATE)** | **4.06 ns/call** | **§13.7 target ≤ 5 ns → ✓ PASS** |
| CM5.1 full dispatch, meta=#f (slow-path bypass) | 4 ns/call | `and` short-circuits at meta=#f |
| CM5.2 full dispatch, meta=sample (full path) | 54 ns/call | **Parameter-ref dominates** |

### Surprise / surfaced finding (the load-bearing 1B-i contribution)

The CM5.2 result was the real surprise. The gate (CM2) passed comfortably. But CM5.2's 54 ns/call is **dominated by the Racket-parameter ref** for the speculation check — `(mock-current-speculation)`. Production currently uses `current-speculation-assumption` (also a parameter) per `metavar-store.rkt:194`. **If 1B-ii implements the cell-write fast-path dispatch using a parameter for the speculation check (matching production's convention), the dispatch overhead jumps from the §13.6 spike's 6.4 ns to ~54 ns — a ~10× regression**.

The §13.6 spike didn't have this problem because the spike's mock used a struct field, not a parameter. Without this 1B-i measurement, the architectural decision (parameter vs struct-field for the speculation check) would have been made implicitly by following production convention — and the spike's PASS verdict would have been quietly invalidated.

The microbench acted as a **design-question surfacer**, not just a gate validator. This is a stronger result than just "A2 is feasible" — it identified an implementation choice that the 1B-i ↔ 1B-ii boundary would otherwise have hidden.

Persisted as architectural recommendation in §9.2.0: **avoid parameter for the speculation check in the cell-write fast path**; lean toward struct field on `prop-net-warm` (where contradiction already lives). Resolve concretely at 1B-ii mini-design.

### Lessons + observations (codification candidates)

- **"Per-phase microbench gates surface design questions, not just gate-target answers"**: 1 data point this commit. The CM5 measurement wasn't strictly necessary for the gate (CM2 was the gate); but including CM5 surfaced a downstream architectural question that the design hadn't pinned down. Watching list — graduates after 1-2 more occurrences. Related to existing "Spike Validates Feasibility, Not Optimality" pattern from this session arc.

- **Printf format-string bugs from embedded `~N` in literal text**: 2 instances in this session (`~6.4 ns` and `~50 ns`). Racket's `printf` interprets `~6` and `~5` as format directives. Mitigation: avoid `~N` in literal text within `printf`/`format`; use "approximately N" instead. Minor — not codification material; just a reminder.

- **Microbench-as-throwaway-prototype pattern continues to pay off**: spike (throwaway code, ~290 LoC) → §13.6 → §13.6.A → §1B-i (throwaway microbench section, ~110 LoC). Each round answers a specific architectural question cheaply before committing production changes. The throwaway-vs-production distinction is structurally important — these benches live in `benchmarks/micro/` and are expected to drift / be replaced as the architecture matures.

### Next: 1B-ii — Specialized cell framework module + dispatch extension

Per §1.2 + §9.2.A + §9.2.B + §9.2.0 surfaced finding:
- Modify `prop-cell` struct: `(value dependents)` → `(value dependents meta)` (`#:transparent` preserved)
- Update ~30 construction sites: add `#f` 3rd arg
- Create `racket/prologos/specialized-cells.rkt` with `cell-meta` struct + `net-register-specialized-cell` API + `cell-meta` field convenience constructors
- Extend `net-cell-write` in `propagator.rkt` with fast-path dispatch (per §9.2.B sketch)
- **§9.2.0 architectural finding**: avoid parameter for speculation check; use struct field on `prop-net-warm` (or pass under-speculation as explicit arg). Decide at 1B-ii mini-design with code in hand.
- Per §13.7 row 1B-ii gate: production W1+ ≤ 10 ns (1.5× spike's 6.4 ns) → PASS

Estimated 1B-ii scope: ~150-250 LoC; ~1h.

### Mempalace state

Microbench-as-design-question-surfacer joins the "Spike Validates Feasibility, Not Optimality" pattern as a watching-list lesson. Next: 1B-ii substrate.

## 2026-05-15 (cont.) — Stage 4 Phase 1B-ii mini-design: 4 architectural questions resolved + §9.2.A inconsistency corrected

Opening 1B-ii per Per-Phase Protocol. The conversational mini-design ran a focused codebase audit on `net-cell-write` (propagator.rkt:1205-1303) which surfaced three NEW architectural questions, in addition to the one carried from 1B-i CM5.2. All four resolved in this conversation; persisted as new §9.2.0.5 in the design doc + §9.2.A correction.

### What the audit surfaced

Reading `net-cell-write` showed it does ~140 ns of work per call: 2 CHAMP lookups (cells + merge-fns) + tagged-cell-value wrapping + struct-copy prop-cell + CHAMP insert + dependent filtering + 2 struct-copy prop-network. The §13.6 spike's W1+ of 6.4 ns measured a **vector-indexed sidecar mock** that bypassed all of this. Production has two architecturally distinct paths to choose from (CHAMP-based vs sidecar) — and the design doc hadn't pinned which.

### The four questions + resolutions

**Q-1B-ii-α (speculation-check mechanism)** — carried from 1B-i CM5.2: parameter ref costs ~50 ns. Resolved to **(A) struct field on `prop-net-warm`** (co-locates with `contradiction`; one struct-field load ~1 ns; on-network; orthogonality-aligned).

**Q-1B-ii-β (specialized cell value storage)** — NEW from this audit. Two architectures:
- CHAMP-based: meta on prop-cell; production W1+ ~50-70 ns; under Option 13 amortization → ~0.5-0.7 ns/cycle (Variant A) or ~2.2 ns/cycle (Variant B, local-var dominates)
- Sidecar: vector-indexed parallel storage; production W1+ ~6-10 ns matching spike; adds dual-source-of-truth complexity

Resolved to **CHAMP-based**. Justified by Option 13 amortization (per-cycle cost is dominated by local-var set-box! in Variant B / propagator fire in Variant A, NOT cell-write); the ~50 ns vs ~10 ns difference at the boundary becomes 0.5 ns vs 0.1 ns per cycle — tiny. Sidecar's dual-storage complexity (invariants under speculation rollback; Phase 3C API split) outweighs the marginal speedup. Per "Let pain drive design": ship the simpler architecture; future PReduce/OE may want the sidecar.

**Q-1B-ii-γ (`specialized-cells.rkt` module shape)** — NEW. The design doc had an internal inconsistency: §9.2.A (drafted at D.4 scaffolding pass) places cell-meta + dispatch hooks in `specialized-cells.rkt`; §9.9 Q-1B-10 (resolved at 2026-05-15 mini-design walkthrough) chose B1-prime (cell-meta in `propagator.rkt`, thin specialized-cells.rkt for convenience). Three shapes were considered:
- Shape 1: no `specialized-cells.rkt`; everything in propagator.rkt
- Shape 2 (B1-prime): cell-meta + API + dispatch in propagator.rkt; thin specialized-cells.rkt for convenience constructors
- Shape 3 (§9.2.A as written): full machinery in specialized-cells.rkt; risks circular imports

Resolved to **Shape 2**. The D.4 §4.6 NTT model frames the framework as canonical infrastructure that future tracks inherit; Shape 1 muddies framework/substrate; Shape 3 has circular-import risk that B1-prime correctly avoided. Shape 2 establishes a proper anchor with `make-monotone-counter-meta` + `make-cold-general-meta` convenience constructors for the two specialized cells (fuel-cost + fuel-budget) Phase 1B/1C ships. ~20-40 LoC thin layer.

**§9.2.A correction**: drive-by edit in this commit. The §9.2.A section header changed from "Specialized cell type framework module" to "Cell mechanism extension (Shape 2)"; Module/Imports/Provides restructured into a two-module split table.

**Q-1B-ii-δ (§13.7 1B-ii gate target)** — NEW. The prior gate "Production W1+ ≤ 10 ns" was set before Option 13 was discovered and assumed the vector-indexed sidecar. Under Option 13 + Q-1B-ii-β CHAMP-based, the load-bearing measurement is per-cycle amortized cost, not per-call W1+. Resolved to **per-cycle amortized ≤ 3 ns at N=100 fires/round (primary, load-bearing); W1+ documented as informational (~50-70 ns expected; absorbed by amortization)**.

### Drift risks named for 1B-ii implementation

- D-1B-ii-1: Backward compat under 3-arg `prop-cell` constructor — ~30 sites must pass `#f` for `meta`; mechanical batch via 2-pass sed pattern; verify zero sites missed (full suite green)
- D-1B-ii-2: Speculation-state field on `prop-net-warm` — must be updated by speculation entry/exit machinery; need to audit at implementation time WHERE speculation is entered/exited in current code
- D-1B-ii-3: On-write predicate allocation — closure must be 0-allocation under fixnum comparison; verify via bench-mem
- D-1B-ii-4: Fire-on-threshold-crossing semantics — skipping notify-dependents most writes; verify Phase 3C consumers still see crossing notifications

### Process observation

This is the second time in the addendum that a mini-design's codebase audit surfaced architectural questions the prior design didn't pin down (first time: §10.3.A audit correction → parallel BSP variant assignment matrix on 2026-05-15). Pattern: **the per-phase audit step routinely catches design-doc handwaves that survived earlier critique rounds because the rounds weren't grounded in the specific code path**. Watching-list candidate alongside "Microbench surfaces design questions" from 1B-i.

### Next: enter 1B-ii Stage 4 implementation (audit + implement)

Per Per-Phase Protocol the next step is the mini-audit codebase pass (sub-phase 2 of step 2; this commit captured the design-side; the implementation-side audit now reads exact prop-cell construction sites, speculation entry/exit points, prop-net-warm current layout, etc.). Then implementation per the resolutions.

### Mempalace state

Unchanged.

🫡 1B-ii mini-design resolutions locked in. 4 architectural questions decided; §9.2.A inconsistency corrected; §13.7 1B-ii gate revised under Option 13 reality. The conversational audit + persistence into design doc keeps the doc consistent with the in-flight decisions. Next commit: 1B-ii implementation under the locked-in choices.

## 2026-05-15 (cont.) — Stage 4 Phase 1B-ii implementation: ✓ §13.7 PRIMARY GATE PASS (2.98 ns/cycle); 8257 tests / 0 failures

Implementation entered Stage 4 per Per-Phase Protocol audit-precedes-implementation discipline. The conversational audit was richer than expected — surfaced a meaningful refinement to Q-1B-ii-α (audit revealed adding a struct field to prop-net-warm was less invasive than initially scoped: `struct-copy` auto-preserves fields so 19 sites need NO change; the speculation-machinery refactor concern was real but a scheduler-refresh-cache approach avoids it entirely).

### Refined Q-1B-ii-α resolution (post-audit)

Initial conversation resolved to A (struct field on prop-net-warm). Audit found that path requires either (A1) refactoring 8 `parameterize` binding sites for `current-worldview-bitmask` to thread the network, OR (A2) keeping `parameterize` machinery unchanged and having the BSP scheduler refresh the field from the parameter at round entry. **A2 (scheduler-refresh) is cleaner**: ~5 LoC at one site; existing speculation machinery untouched; cache freshness aligned with the scheduler-state-cell constraint (specialized cells are scheduler-state cells written at round boundaries; the cache freshness window matches).

User confirmed A IS more principled than the A' (re-use cell-id 1 worldview-cache) alternative I floated; the audit cost was overstated. Persisted refinement to design doc §9.2.0.5 Q-1B-ii-α.

### What was implemented

**Struct changes** (propagator.rkt:253, 336):
- `prop-cell` gained 3rd field `meta` — defaults `#f` for regular cells; `specialized-cell-meta` value for specialized cells
- `prop-net-warm` gained 3rd field `under-speculation?` — scheduler-maintained cache of `(current-worldview-bitmask)`

**Construction site updates**:
- 17 `prop-cell` direct constructions updated to 3-arg with `#f` default (mechanical batch via `replace_all` patterns)
- 2 `prop-net-warm` direct constructions updated to 3-arg with `#f` defaults
- 19 `struct-copy prop-net-warm` sites needed NO change (auto-preserve)
- ~5+ `struct-copy prop-cell` sites needed NO change (auto-preserve)

**Cell-meta machinery** (propagator.rkt):
- New struct `specialized-cell-meta` with 5 fields (tier, storage, fires-on, on-write-check, on-read-check). Renamed from `cell-meta` after compile-time collision with `prop-observatory.rkt`'s pre-existing observability struct.
- New API `net-register-specialized-cell` (~30 LoC) mirroring `net-new-cell` shape but storing cell-meta on the prop-cell
- New fast-path branch at top of `net-cell-write` (~50 LoC): if meta + hot + monotone-counter + not under speculation, run on-write-check inline, write contradiction structurally OR apply fire-on policy; else fall through to existing path
- Slow-path body extracted as `net-cell-write/slow-path` helper (zero behavioral change; just a structural extraction so the cond-arm can short-circuit cleanly)
- BSP scheduler `run-to-quiescence-bsp` round-entry snapshot construction extended with `[under-speculation? (not (zero? (current-worldview-bitmask)))]` (~5 LoC at one site)
- New module `racket/prologos/specialized-cells.rkt` (~50 LoC; Shape 2 per Q-1B-ii-γ): re-exports `specialized-cell-meta` + `net-register-specialized-cell` + convenience constructors `make-monotone-counter-meta` and `make-cold-general-meta`

**Tests** (tests/test-specialized-cells.rkt; 9 tests; all pass):
1. cell-meta struct construction + accessors
2. backward compat: regular cells (meta=#f) take slow path unchanged
3. net-register-specialized-cell attaches cell-meta
4. fast-path: write below threshold preserves cell value, no contradiction
5. on-write-check fires inline → structural contradiction
6. prop-net-warm.under-speculation? defaults to #f
7. fast-path same-value write returns same network (termination)
8. cold+general specialized cell falls through to slow path (tier != 'hot)
9. make-prop-network smoke test (regular cells work post-prop-cell-field addition)

**§13.7 PRIMARY GATE microbench** (bench-ppn-track4c.rkt; new CW1-CW3 section):
- CW1 generic cell-write (slow path; meta=#f): 110 ns/call (note: most iterations hit "no-change return" path under max-merge with repeated writes)
- CW2 specialized cell-write (fast path; production CHAMP-based): **256 ns/call** — significantly higher than the §13.7 secondary estimate of 50-70 ns
- CW3 round-of-N=100 (1 cell-write + 100 local-var set-box! mutations): 298 ns total
- **Per-cycle amortized: 2.98 ns/cycle — ✓ PASS** (gate ≤ 3 ns; at the boundary)

### Surfaced findings + observations

**1. Name collision: `cell-meta` already exists** — `prop-observatory.rkt` (pre-existing) exports `cell-meta` as an observability struct (`(subsystem label source-loc domain extra)`). My new framework struct collided. Renamed to `specialized-cell-meta`. Useful precedent: name struct types explicitly when they live alongside other "meta" concepts; "cell-meta" alone was too generic.

**2. Production W1+ (CW2) = 256 ns, NOT 50-70 ns as design estimated** — the §13.7 1B-ii revised gate said "production W1+ expected 50-70 ns under CHAMP-based; absorbed by Option 13 amortization." Actual measurement: 256 ns. The CHAMP ops (champ-lookup + champ-insert) + struct-copy prop-cell + struct-copy prop-network all add up. The Option 13 amortization still absorbs this (CW3 amortized = 2.98 ns/cycle), but the boundary is close. **Codification candidate**: when a design's quantitative gate has a "secondary informational expected X" annotation, the actual value should be measured (per §13.7 reality), not assumed. Watching: 1 data point.

**3. §13.7 GATE PASSES BY A THIN MARGIN** — 2.98 ns/cycle vs 3.0 ns/cycle target. Could trip on noise across runs. If a future change increases CW2, the gate would fail. **Phase 1V close should re-microbench CW3 multiple times for stability**. Noted as a 1V watchitem.

**4. Microbench-as-design-question-surfacer pattern continues** — the 1B-i CM5.2 measurement specifically motivated Q-1B-ii-α (avoid parameter ref for speculation check). This 1B-ii CW2 measurement specifically motivates the next observation about §13.7 reality. Each microbench round surfaces a concrete architectural reality that the design's prose handwaved. Pattern: 3 data points now (CM5.2; §13.6 spike; §13.6.A spike) — strong watching-list candidate.

**5. Mock simplification caveat for CW3** — my simulate-bsp-round-N100 uses `set-box!` per "fire" (~2 ns each). Real production "fires" are propagator invocations (~100-1000 ns each). Under real workload, the per-cycle amortized cost would be DOMINATED by the actual fire cost, not the cell-write. The 256 ns cell-write amortizes to ~0.025 ns/cycle when fires are 1 μs each. The CW3 measurement (2.98 ns/cycle) is a worst-case bound that real workload won't hit.

**6. The slow-path helper extraction was zero-behavior-change** — verified empirically by full suite GREEN (8257 tests; 0 failures). Confirms the refactor preserves existing net-cell-write semantics for all non-specialized cells (which is every cell currently registered in production — since Phase 1C hasn't shipped yet to register any specialized cells).

### Suite state

- Pre-1B-ii baseline: 7914 tests / 119.3s (per S1 baseline 2026-04-26)
- Post-1B-ii: 8257 tests / 114.5s
- Net: +343 tests (9 new + others from session arc), -4.8s wall time (within noise band; not a regression)

### Vision Alignment Gate (1B-ii)

Adversarial TWO-COLUMN per workflow.md (catalogue + challenge):

| Question | Catalogue | Challenge |
|---|---|---|
| (a) On-network? | ✓ cell-meta on prop-cell; under-speculation? on prop-net-warm; both on-network. | Challenge: is the BSP scheduler refresh of under-speculation? "on-network"? It reads a parameter and writes a struct field. The PARAMETER itself is ambient state — but we're not reading it inside the fast-path (which would have been ~50 ns); we're caching to the field. So the cell-write fast-path is on-network; the cache-refresh happens once per round at a known boundary. **Pass with constraint named** (per §9.2.0.5 α A2 constraint). |
| (b) Complete? | ✓ all 4 deliverables shipped (prop-cell field; prop-net-warm field + refresh; cell-meta + API + dispatch; specialized-cells.rkt); 9 tests pass; gate passes. | Challenge: did the design's quantitative claims land? The §13.7 SECONDARY estimate of "W1+ 50-70 ns" was UNDER by ~4×. The PRIMARY gate (2.98 ns) passed but at boundary. **Pass with surfaced reality** — secondary estimate was wrong; primary gate held. |
| (c) Vision-advancing? | ✓ first specialized cell type framework lands; future PReduce / OE / SH tracks can use `net-register-specialized-cell` + `make-*-meta` convenience layer. | Challenge: is the framework actually usable by future tracks? The fast-path is HARDCODED to `'hot` + `'monotone-counter` + `'threshold-crossing` — other combinations (e.g., `'sparse` storage, `'monotonic-progress` fire-on) don't take fast paths. Per "Let pain drive design" this is fine; future tracks extend per concrete need. **Pass**. |
| (d) Drift-risks-cleared? | D-1B-ii-1 (backward compat) ✓ via full suite green. D-1B-ii-2 (speculation field updated) ✓ via BSP scheduler refresh + test 6. D-1B-ii-3 (predicate allocation) — not measured; closure capture is on-write-check + budget; worth re-verifying. D-1B-ii-4 (fire-on-threshold-crossing) ✓ via test 5. | Challenge: D-1B-ii-3 (predicate allocation) wasn't directly tested. The on-write-check IS a closure — for fuel-cost cell registration in Phase 1C, the closure will capture budget (constant per cell). Should be 0-allocation under fixnum comparison. **Partial — verify at Phase 1C registration with allocation profile.** |

VAG result: PASS with named caveats. The 1B-ii framework is shipped; Phase 1C close should verify D-1B-ii-3 (allocation) at fuel-cost cell registration.

### Lessons + observations (codification candidates)

- **"Per-phase microbench gates surface design questions, not just gate-target answers"** — now 3 data points in this addendum (1B-i CM5.2; 1B-ii CW2; both §13.6 spikes). Strong codification candidate; surfacing at next track-close or PIR.

- **"Microbench discipline catches design's quantitative claims that handwaved"** — the §13.7 secondary estimate of W1+ 50-70 ns was empirically ~256 ns. Without measuring, would have silently shipped with the wrong assumption baked into future design decisions. Watching: 1 data point.

- **"Struct-copy in Racket auto-preserves unspecified fields"** — this is well-known but I'd FORGOTTEN it during the audit; that's why I overstated the scope of A (19 struct-copy sites needing updates). Re-internalized; future audits should check the language semantic before estimating scope. Not codification-worthy; just a reminder.

- **"Compile-time collision-detection in Racket catches naming conflicts cleanly"** — `raco make` gave a precise error "identifier already required" pointing at both modules. Rename + recompile clean. The compile-error message was load-bearing for the diagnosis. Good Racket UX.

### Next: 1B-iii — Tropical fuel module (merge/tensor/residuation + SRE registration + C-series)

Per §9.2 + §9.3 + §9.4. Estimated scope: ~150-250 LoC + C-series quantale axiom tests. Need to settle merge semantic (max vs min vs replace) — surfaced during 1B-ii implementation reading that the design's `tropical-fuel-merge = min` doesn't operationally accumulate; for fuel-cost cell with `(+ current n)` writes, merge needs to be `max` (or the cell stores remaining-fuel decrementing). Lean: settle at 1B-iii mini-design when the actual cell registration is on the table.

Unchanged.

## 2026-05-16 — Stage 4 Phase 1B-iii mini-design: 4 architectural questions resolved (cost-vs-remaining + SRE registration scope + C-series + API naming)

Opening 1B-iii per Per-Phase Protocol. The mini-design conversation surfaced one load-bearing decision (cost vs remaining-fuel framing) plus three lower-stakes confirmations. All four resolved; persisted as new §9.2.0.6 in the design doc.

### The load-bearing question (Q-1B-iii-α)

The design had an internal tension: §9.3 stated `tropical-fuel-merge = min` (Lawvere join), but §10.3 D.4's Phase 1C migration patterns used `(net-cell-write net cid (+ current n))` — which under min-merge gets silently no-op'd (the writes are dropped). Two framings considered:

| Option | Cell semantic | Merge | Initial | Writes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | Remaining fuel (counts down) | `min` (Lawvere join) | budget | `(- current n)` |
| B | Accumulated cost (counts up) | `max` (Lawvere meet) or replace | 0 | `(+ current n)` |

Resolved to **Option A**. Justified by:
- 1:1 match with existing native fuel mechanism
- Algebraic correctness under Lawvere convention (min as join)
- Smaller Phase 1C migration footprint
- The cost framing remains as a derived concept (cost = budget - remaining)

§10.3 D.4 examples become wrong under Option A (they use `(+ current n)`); flagged as 1B-iv consistency item.

### Subtle architectural clarification: SRE-domain contradicts? vs cell on-write-check

The audit-conversation surfaced a separation I hadn't named before:
- **SRE domain `contradicts?`** = "is this value the algebraic top of the lattice?" — captures the abstract algebra (Lawvere top = `+inf.0`)
- **Cell `on-write-check`** = "does this write trigger the operational threshold?" — captures cell-specific operational semantics (for remaining-fuel: `(<= new 0)`)

These two predicates serve different purposes and both are needed. The SRE domain stays algebraically clean (contradicts?=(= v +inf.0) per §9.4); the cell instance overlays operational semantics via on-write-check at registration time. 1B-iii ships the abstract quantale; 1B-iv applies it.

This separation maps onto the broader principle: SRE domains describe ABSTRACT ALGEBRA; specific cells overlay OPERATIONAL SEMANTICS. Worth flagging as a watching-list lesson: "abstract-vs-operational separation cleanly distinguishes SRE domain config from cell config."

### User-flagged consideration on β

User noted: "This should be something we can test for with our SRE algebraic mechanisms/property testing." SRE Track 2I shipped property-cell infrastructure + `all-sweep-properties` sweeps. The tropical-fuel quantale's property declarations can be empirically verified via the same sweeps. Tracked as a 1B-iii post-impl consideration: after the domain is registered, run a sweep to confirm declared properties hold empirically. If Track 2I infrastructure isn't ready or readily applicable here, defer to 1B-iv close or Phase 1V close.

### Resolutions summary

- **α**: Option A (remaining fuel; decrement) — algebraic + operational consistency
- **β**: Full quantale property declarations + meet function (max) for downstream consumers
- **γ**: C1+C2+C3 axioms in 1B-iii; C4+C5 deferred to 1B-iv (where cells exist)
- **δ**: API naming per §9.2 Provides locked in; `+inf.0` representation; residuation as read-time helper

### Drift risks named

- D-1B-iii-1: Numerical edge cases at `+inf.0` (distributivity, residuation, tensor identity)
- D-1B-iii-2: Adjunction law correctness for residuation operator
- D-1B-iii-3: merge-registry function signature (returns #f for unknown relations)
- D-1B-iii-4: **1B-iv consistency item** — §10.3 D.4 example using `(+ current n)` is wrong under Option A; correct at 1B-iv
- D-1B-iii-5: SRE property-test sweep on new domain — verify post-impl

### Next: 1B-iii implementation

Implementation plan (per §9.2 + §9.3 + §9.4 + §9.6):
- Create `racket/prologos/tropical-fuel.rkt` with module header documenting remaining-fuel semantic
- Lattice constants + algebraic primitives (merge, meet, tensor, contradiction?, left-residual)
- merge-registry constructor (function relation-name → merge-fn)
- SRE domain registration via `make-sre-domain` with full quantale properties + operations
- `register-domain!` + `register-merge-fn!/lattice` calls at top-level
- Tests `tests/test-tropical-fuel.rkt`: merge + tensor + residuation + contradiction + SRE lookup + C1+C2+C3 axioms

Estimated scope: ~135-185 LoC code + ~100-150 LoC tests.

### Mempalace state

Unchanged.

🫡 1B-iii mini-design resolutions locked in. Option A (remaining fuel) keeps the cell mechanism aligned with both Lawvere algebra AND existing native fuel mechanism. SRE domain ships as abstract quantale; cell-specific operational semantics overlay at 1B-iv. Implementation per Stage 4 next.
